Not really in the way I used to, no. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m miserable every second or anything… but for a long time now most days have had that gray, flat feeling.

Fishing, the tomatoes, a church concert—those are probably the closest things. And sometimes I’m glad I did them. But looking forward to them and really enjoying them without that “going through the motions” feeling… that’s been pretty rare. A lot of the time it’s more like I’m just doing the right things, checking them off, if that makes sense.

I wasn’t sure if that was enough of a reason to come in, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I still do them, mostly. I mean, I go fishing sometimes, I tend my tomatoes, I’ll go to a church concert if there’s one on… so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But if I’m being honest, for a long time now it’s felt kind of gray and flat most days. Things I used to look forward to feel more like something I’m checking off a list than something I’m really enjoying. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming gray and flat?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say it really started around when I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was about two years and four months ago, I think. I kept telling myself it was just the responsibility and that I was handling it fine, but the gray feeling has kind of stuck around since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: A few, I guess… I still go fishing sometimes, and I mess around with the tomatoes in the garden, and I’ll go to a church concert now and then. So I don’t want to make it sound like I get no enjoyment at all.\n\nBut… for a long time now, most days it’s had this gray, flat feeling to it. Even the things I used to really look forward to can feel more like I’m just checking them off a list than actually enjoying them. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do some of them. But fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—those used to be things I’d really look forward to.\n\nNow a lot of the time they just feel kind of… gray, I guess. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, or because I should, more than because I’m really getting pleasure out of them. More like checking something off a list, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like there’s nothing. I can still get something out of fishing, or tending the tomatoes, or a church concert once in a while.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, most days it’s still pretty gray and flat. Those things used to feel like something I looked forward to, and now a lot of the time they feel more like… items on a checklist. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, not because I’m really enjoying them the way I used to. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in and look forward to, without feeling like you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Not really in the way I used to, no. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m miserable every second or anything… but for a long time now most days have had that gray, flat feeling.\n\nFishing, the tomatoes, a church concert—those are probably the closest things. And sometimes I’m glad I did them. But looking forward to them and really enjoying them without that “going through the motions” feeling… that’s been pretty rare. A lot of the time it’s more like I’m just doing the right things, checking them off, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any days where you feel like you're able to enjoy things without that gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: There are probably a few moments here and there, yes… I don’t want to say it’s every single day or that nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut whole days where that gray, flat feeling isn’t there? Not many, if I’m being honest. For a long time now, most days it’s kind of in the background. Even things I used to really look forward to—fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—can still feel more like I’m checking them off a list than really enjoying them.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this gray, flat feeling for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been about two years and four months now, since I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work.\n\nThat’s when I really started noticing that gray, flat feeling settling in most days. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced this gray, flat feeling almost every day since it started?\n\nPatient: Almost every day… yes, I’d say most days. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I can still do things and there are little moments that aren’t terrible.\n\nBut that gray, flat feeling has been there a lot—kind of in the background. Even with things I used to enjoy, like fishing or tending the tomatoes or going to a church concert, it often feels more like something I’m checking off than something I’m really taking pleasure in.\n\nIs that the kind of “almost every day” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have, though I don’t want to make it sound like I never enjoy anything at all.\n\nIt’s more that for a long time now, there’s been this gray, flat feeling most days. Things I used to look forward to—like fishing, tending the tomatoes, even going to a church concert—sometimes feel more like items on a checklist than something I’m really enjoying.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but that’s how it feels.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m trying to think of one… I mean, there are moments, sure. I don’t want to make it sound like everything is awful all the time.

Even the things I used to really look forward to can feel more like I’m just checking them off a list than actually enjoying them. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do some of them. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m miserable every second or anything… but for a long time now most days have had that gray, flat feeling.\n\nFishing, the tomatoes, a church concert—those are probably the closest things. But even before the day really gets going, I feel like I’m already running on a low battery. I wasn’t sure if that was enough of a reason to come in, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I still do them, mostly. I mean, I go fishing sometimes, I tend my tomatoes, I’ll go to a church concert if there’s one on… so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But if I’m being honest, for a long time now it’s felt kind of gray and flat most days. Things I used to look forward to feel more like something I’m checking off a list than something I’m really enjoying. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming gray and flat?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say it really started around when I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was about two years and four months ago, I think. I kept telling myself it was just the responsibility and that I was handling it fine, but the gray feeling has kind of stuck around since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: A few, I guess… I still go fishing sometimes, and I mess around with the tomatoes in the garden, and I’ll go to a church concert now and then. So I don’t want to make it sound like I get no enjoyment at all.\n\nBut… for a long time now, most days it’s had this gray, flat feeling to it. Even the things I used to really look forward to can feel more like I’m just checking them off a list than actually enjoying them. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do some of them. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, or because I should, more than because I’m really getting pleasure out of them. More like checking something off a list, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like there’s nothing. I can still get something out of fishing, or tending the tomatoes, or a church concert once in a while.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, most days it’s still pretty gray and flat. Those things used to feel like something I looked forward to, and now a lot of the time they feel more like… items on a checklist. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, not because I’m really enjoying them the way I used to. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in and look forward to, without feeling like you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Not really in the way I used to, no. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m miserable every second or anything… but for a long time now most days have had that gray, flat feeling.\n\nFishing, the tomatoes, a church concert—those are probably the closest things. And sometimes I’m glad I did them. But looking forward to them and really enjoying them without that “going through the motions” feeling… that’s been pretty rare. A lot of the time it’s more like I’m just doing the right things, checking them off, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any days where you feel like you're able to enjoy things without that gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: There are probably a few moments here and there, yes… I don’t want to say it’s every single day or that nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut whole days where that gray, flat feeling isn’t there? But if I’m being honest, for a long time now it’s felt kind of gray and flat most days. Things I used to look forward to feel more like something I’m checking off a list than something I’m really enjoying. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming gray and flat?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say it really started around when I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was about two years and four months ago, I think. I kept telling myself it was just the responsibility and that I was handling it fine, but the gray feeling has kind of stuck around since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: A few, I guess… I still go fishing sometimes, and I mess around with the tomatoes in the garden, and I’ll go to a church concert now and then. Even the things I used to really look forward to can feel more like I’m just checking them off a list than actually enjoying them. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do some of them. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, or because I should, more than because I’m really getting pleasure out of them. More like checking something off a list, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like there’s nothing. A lot of the time it’s more like I’m just doing the right things, checking them off, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any days where you feel like you're able to enjoy things without that gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: There are probably a few moments here and there, yes… I don’t want to say it’s every single day or that nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut whole days where that gray, flat feeling isn’t there? But even before the day really gets going, I feel like I’m already running on a low battery. I wasn’t sure if that was enough of a reason to come in, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I still do them, mostly. I mean, I go fishing sometimes, I tend my tomatoes, I’ll go to a church concert if there’s one on… so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But if I’m being honest, for a long time now it’s felt kind of gray and flat most days. Things I used to look forward to feel more like something I’m checking off a list than something I’m really enjoying. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming gray and flat?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say it really started around when I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was about two years and four months ago, I think. I kept telling myself it was just the responsibility and that I was handling it fine, but the gray feeling has kind of stuck around since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: A few, I guess… I still go fishing sometimes, and I mess around with the tomatoes in the garden, and I’ll go to a church concert now and then. So I don’t want to make it sound like I get no enjoyment at all.\n\nBut… for a long time now, most days it’s had this gray, flat feeling to it. Even the things I used to really look forward to can feel more like I’m just checking them off a list than actually enjoying them. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do some of them. But fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—those used to be things I’d really look forward to.\n\nNow a lot of the time they just feel kind of… gray, I guess. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, or because I should, more than because I’m really getting pleasure out of them. More like checking something off a list, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like there’s nothing. I can still get something out of fishing, or tending the tomatoes, or a church concert once in a while.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, most days it’s still pretty gray and flat. Those things used to feel like something I looked forward to, and now a lot of the time they feel more like… items on a checklist. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, not because I’m really enjoying them the way I used to. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in and look forward to, without feeling like you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Not really in the way I used to, no. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m miserable every second or anything… but for a long time now most days have had that gray, flat feeling.\n\nFishing, the tomatoes, a church concert—those are probably the closest things. And sometimes I’m glad I did them. But looking forward to them and really enjoying them without that “going through the motions” feeling… that’s been pretty rare. A lot of the time it’s more like I’m just doing the right things, checking them off, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any days where you feel like you're able to enjoy things without that gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: There are probably a few moments here and there, yes… I don’t want to say it’s every single day or that nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut whole days where that gray, flat feeling isn’t there? Not many, if I’m being honest. For a long time now, most days it’s kind of in the background. Even things I used to really look forward to—fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—can still feel more like I’m checking them off a list than really enjoying them.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this gray, flat feeling for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been about two years and four months now, since I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work.\n\nThat’s when I really started noticing that gray, flat feeling settling in most days. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced this gray, flat feeling almost every day since it started?\n\nPatient: Almost every day… yes, I’d say most days. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I can still do things and there are little moments that aren’t terrible.\n\nBut that gray, flat feeling has been there a lot—kind of in the background. Even with things I used to enjoy, like fishing or tending the tomatoes or going to a church concert, it often feels more like something I’m checking off than something I’m really taking pleasure in.\n\nIs that the kind of “almost every day” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have, though I don’t want to make it sound like I never enjoy anything at all.\n\nIt’s more that for a long time now, there’s been this gray, flat feeling most days. Things I used to look forward to—like fishing, tending the tomatoes, even going to a church concert—sometimes feel more like items on a checklist than something I’m really enjoying.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess… I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything.\n\nMy appetite’s just been off more days than not. Does that count as significant?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I can sleep, and I’m still keeping up with work okay, so it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything. But even before the day really gets going, I feel like I’m already running on a low battery. I wasn’t sure if that was enough of a reason to come in, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I still do them, mostly. I mean, I go fishing sometimes, I tend my tomatoes, I’ll go to a church concert if there’s one on… so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But if I’m being honest, for a long time now it’s felt kind of gray and flat most days. Things I used to look forward to feel more like something I’m checking off a list than something I’m really enjoying. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with things seeming gray and flat?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. I’d say it really started around when I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was about two years and four months ago, I think. I kept telling myself it was just the responsibility and that I was handling it fine, but the gray feeling has kind of stuck around since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: A few, I guess… I still go fishing sometimes, and I mess around with the tomatoes in the garden, and I’ll go to a church concert now and then. So I don’t want to make it sound like I get no enjoyment at all.\n\nBut… for a long time now, most days it’s had this gray, flat feeling to it. Even the things I used to really look forward to can feel more like I’m just checking them off a list than actually enjoying them. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do some of them. But fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—those used to be things I’d really look forward to.\n\nNow a lot of the time they just feel kind of… gray, I guess. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, or because I should, more than because I’m really getting pleasure out of them. More like checking something off a list, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like there’s nothing. I can still get something out of fishing, or tending the tomatoes, or a church concert once in a while.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, most days it’s still pretty gray and flat. Those things used to feel like something I looked forward to, and now a lot of the time they feel more like… items on a checklist. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, not because I’m really enjoying them the way I used to. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in and look forward to, without feeling like you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Not really in the way I used to, no. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m miserable every second or anything… but for a long time now most days have had that gray, flat feeling.\n\nFishing, the tomatoes, a church concert—those are probably the closest things. And sometimes I’m glad I did them. But looking forward to them and really enjoying them without that “going through the motions” feeling… that’s been pretty rare. A lot of the time it’s more like I’m just doing the right things, checking them off, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any days where you feel like you're able to enjoy things without that gray, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: There are probably a few moments here and there, yes… I don’t want to say it’s every single day or that nothing ever feels good.\n\nBut whole days where that gray, flat feeling isn’t there? Not many, if I’m being honest. For a long time now, most days it’s kind of in the background. Even things I used to really look forward to—fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—can still feel more like I’m checking them off a list than really enjoying them.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this gray, flat feeling for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been about two years and four months now, since I got pulled back into full operational leadership at work.\n\nThat’s when I really started noticing that gray, flat feeling settling in most days. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced this gray, flat feeling almost every day since it started?\n\nPatient: Almost every day… yes, I’d say most days. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I can still do things and there are little moments that aren’t terrible.\n\nBut that gray, flat feeling has been there a lot—kind of in the background. Even with things I used to enjoy, like fishing or tending the tomatoes or going to a church concert, it often feels more like something I’m checking off than something I’m really taking pleasure in.\n\nIs that the kind of “almost every day” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have, though I don’t want to make it sound like I never enjoy anything at all.\n\nIt’s more that for a long time now, there’s been this gray, flat feeling most days. Things I used to look forward to—like fishing, tending the tomatoes, even going to a church concert—sometimes feel more like items on a checklist than something I’m really enjoying.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I guess… I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything.\n\nMy appetite’s just been off more days than not. I’ll get busy and work through lunch without really noticing, and then at dinner I kind of pick at it. My wife’s mentioned my pants are fitting looser, so… I suppose there has been some weight change. Does that count as significant?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying recently?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I know I’m getting older, so the subject crosses your mind now and then in a normal way, I suppose.\n\nBut I haven’t been sitting around thinking about death or wanting to die, if that’s what you mean. At least, I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and still enjoy without feeling like you're just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one… I mean, there are moments, sure. I don’t want to make it sound like everything is awful all the time.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, even the things I’d normally say I enjoy—fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—most of the time they’ve got that same sort of gray, flat feeling around them. Like I’m doing them because they’re things I do, not because I’m really looking forward to them.\n\nMaybe every now and then I get a little spark of it, but it doesn’t seem to last. But fishing, working with the tomatoes, going to a church concert—those used to be things I’d really look forward to.\n\nNow a lot of the time they just feel kind of… gray, I guess. Like I’m doing them because they’re good things to do, or because I should, more than because I’m really getting pleasure out of them. More like checking something off a list, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like there’s nothing.
